# CHAPTER 50
## GONGSHU BAN <note>original: "KUNG SHU"</note>

Gongshu Ban <note>original: "Kung Shu Pan"</note> had completed the construction of "cloud-ladders" <note>large siege ladders used to scale city walls</note> for the state of Chu <note>original: "Ch'u"</note> and was preparing to attack the state of Song <note>original: "Sung"</note> with them. Mozi <note>original: "Motse"</note> heard of this and set out from the state of Qi <note>original: "Ch'i"</note>. He walked for ten days and ten nights¹ and arrived at the city of Ying.² He went to see Gongshu Ban. The inventor asked him what he wanted from him. Mozi said: "Someone in the north has humiliated me. I would like to hire you to kill him." Gongshu Ban was displeased. Mozi persisted, offering him ten *jin* <note>original: "chin"; a unit of weight often used for gold or currency</note>.³ Finally, Gongshu Ban said: "My moral principles are incompatible with murdering people."

Upon hearing this, Mozi rose and bowed twice ⁴ and spoke: "Now, let me explain myself. While I was in the north, I heard you were building ladders to attack the state of Song. Now, what crime is Song guilty of? The state of Chu <note>original: "Ching"</note> ⁵ has land to spare but is short of people. To destroy what is scarce <note>human life</note> in order to strive for what is already plentiful <note>territory</note> cannot be called wise. Since Song is innocent, to attack it cannot be called righteous. To fail to make an effort to argue against this according to what you know to be right cannot be called loyal. To make the effort of building weapons without obtaining the desired result of peace cannot be called effective. To hold a principle that forbids the killing of one person while assisting in the killing of many—"

---

¹ This story is told in other historical works as well. It was said that Mozi had to tear off parts of his clothing to wrap his feet to endure this continuous, long journey.
² Ying was the capital of the state of Chu, located in what is now Hubei Province.
³ See page 214, note 2 <note>refers to ancient Chinese currency measurements</note>.
⁴ This bowing is a formal expression of apology or deep respect.
⁵ "Ching" and "Chu" are two different names for the same powerful southern state.

<vocab>Gongshu Ban, Chu, Song, Mozi, Qi, Ying, Cloud-ladders, jin, Hubei Province</vocab>

<summary>Mozi travels a great distance to confront the inventor Gongshu Ban, using a clever rhetorical trap to point out the hypocrisy of refusing to kill one man while building weapons to destroy an entire innocent state.</summary>
<keywords>Mozi, Gongshu Ban, just war, cloud-ladders, pacifism, hypocrisy, siege warfare, State of Chu, State of Song</keywords>

<header>THE WORKS OF MOZI <note>original: "MOTSE"</note></header>

—[to forbid the killing of a] few while allowing the killing of many cannot be said to be understanding the fundamental principles of logic <note>original: "fundamental categories"</note>."

Gongshu Ban <note>original: "Kung Shu Pan"</note> was convinced. Mozi <note>original: "Motse"</note> urged him further: "Then why will you not stop the attack?" Gongshu Ban said that it could not be done, as he had already promised the Lord of the state. Mozi said: "Why not then present me to the Lord?" Gongshu Ban agreed.

Mozi saw the Lord and said: "Suppose there is a man who, putting aside his own elegant carriage, desires to steal his neighbor’s broken <note>original: "shattered"</note> sedan chair; putting aside his own fine embroidery and silks, desires to steal his neighbor’s short, coarse jacket; putting aside his own meat and grains, desires to steal his neighbor’s husks <note>the indigestible outer shells of grain; a sign of extreme poverty</note>. What kind of a man would this be?" The Lord said that the man must be suffering from a compulsive urge to steal <note>original: "kleptomania"</note>. 

Mozi continued: "The land of Chu <note>original: "Ching"</note> covers five thousand *li* <note>a Chinese unit of distance, roughly one-third of a mile</note> square, while that of Song <note>original: "Sung"</note> is only five hundred. This is similar to the contrast between the elegant carriage and the broken sedan chair. Chu possesses the Yunmeng Marsh, which is full of rhinoceroses and deer. The fish, tortoises, and crocodiles in the Yangtze <note>original: "Yangtse"</note> and the Han Rivers are the wealthiest resources in the empire. Meanwhile, Song is said to possess not even pheasants, rabbits, or foxes. This is similar to the contrast between fine meat and grains versus husks. In Chu, there are tall pines, spruces, cedars, and camphor trees, while Song has no tall trees at all. This is similar to the contrast between fine embroidery and the short jacket. When your ministers and generals set out to attack Song, it seems to me to be the same kind of comparison. I can see, my Lord, that you will be violating righteousness to no advantage."

The Lord said: "That is all very well. But Gongshu Ban..."

<summary>Mozi uses a series of analogies to convince the King of Chu that attacking the small, resource-poor state of Song would be as irrational and "insane" as a wealthy man stealing scraps from a beggar.</summary>
<keywords>Mozi, Gongshu Ban, Chu, Song, diplomacy, anti-war rhetoric, righteousness, Yunmeng Marsh</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the story of Mozi’s diplomatic mission to the state of Chu. After demonstrating that the state of Song cannot be easily conquered, Mozi successfully convinces the King to call off the invasion, though he receives no thanks from the people he saved.</meta>

<page-num>259</page-num>

# GONGSHU <note>original: "KUNG SHU"</note>

The King said, "Gongshu Ban <note>original: "Pan"</note> has already constructed the 'Cloud-ladders' <note>massive siege ladders used to scale high city walls</note> for me, and I must capture Song <note>original: "Sung"</note>." He then turned to Gongshu Ban.

Mozi <note>original: "Motse"</note> untied his belt and laid it out to represent a city wall, and used a small stick to represent weapons. Gongshu Ban set up nine different types of siege engines <note>original: "machines of attack"</note>. Mozi repulsed him nine times. Gongshu Ban had exhausted all his strategies for attack, while Mozi was far from being exhausted in his methods of defense.

Gongshu Ban felt embarrassed and declared: “I know how I can defeat you, but I will not say.” Mozi also said: “I know how you intend to defeat me, but I will not say.” The King <note>original: "Lord"</note> of Chu asked what he meant. Mozi replied: “Gongshu Ban’s idea is simply to have me murdered. He believes that once I am killed, Song would be powerless to defend itself, and would fall to your attack. However, my disciples, Qin Huali <note>original: "Ch’in Hua Li"</note> and three hundred others, are already armed with my defensive inventions and are waiting on the city walls of Song for the invaders <note>original: "bandits"</note> from Chu. Even if I am murdered, you cannot overcome the defense of Song.” The King of Chu said: “Well, then let us not attack Song any more.”

On his way back <note>likely returning to the states of Qi or Lu</note>, Mozi passed through Song. It was raining, and he sought shelter at a city gate <note>original: "pass"</note>. However, the guard of the gate would not let him in. Thus it is said: “The merit of the man who does his work in secret <note>original: "cultivates himself before the spirits," meaning to work behind the scenes or in the dark</note> is not recognized by the common people. On the other hand, he who performs his deeds in public is the one who is praised.”

<summary>Mozi defeats Gongshu Ban in a tactical simulation and reveals that his 300 students are already prepared to defend Song, successfully deterring the King of Chu from going to war. The text concludes with a lesson on the unrecognized nature of true virtue, as Mozi is denied shelter by the very people he saved.</summary>

<keywords>Mozi, Gongshu Ban, Chu, Song, pacifism, siege warfare, Qin Huali, Cloud-ladders, defense vs offense, hidden merit</keywords>
